2012年埃塞俄比亚全国免疫覆盖率调查

2012 Ethiopian national immunization coverage survey
作者： Coverage Survey Task Force, Habtamu Teklie, Theodros Getachew, James McQuen Patterson, Gavin Grant, François Gasse, Dale Rhoda
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究旨在评估 12 至 23 个月儿童所有抗原的免疫覆盖率以及出生时免受破伤风感染的儿童比例,因为准确的覆盖率估计对于规划决策和改进免疫工作至关重要。
设计与对象
2012年免疫覆盖率调查是一项多阶段分层整群设计横断面全国调查,在11个地区的550个整群中进行,针对12-23个月的儿童和0-11个月儿童的母亲。该调查包括家庭访谈和医疗机构工作人员问卷调查。
核心方法
调查采用整群抽样方法,根据整群间和整群内的变异性,为两个组(破伤风免疫组和婴儿免疫组)各选择7户家庭。免疫覆盖率被定义为通过卡片、护理人员历史或 EPI 登记册报告的免疫情况,并使用免疫卡中的退出率对数据进行调整,以估计真实的覆盖率。
主要结果
按抗原加权的 EPI 覆盖率为 BCG 79.6%、DPT-HepB-Hib1 80.0%、OPV1 90.1%、调整后的 DPT-HepB-Hib3 65.7%、OPV3 70.5% 和 麻疹 68.2%。全国未经调整的 DPT-HepB-Hib1-3 退出率为 25.6%,范围从亚的斯亚贝巴的 2.6% 到索马里的 63.8%。根据世界卫生组织的定义,68% 的出生儿童不会感染新生儿破伤风。
解释与创新
研究结果强调了免疫计划中的显着退出率,并表明通过护理人员回忆收集的数据可能不如免疫卡或 EPI 登记册中的数据准确。调整后的覆盖率估计值提供了更可靠的免疫接种成功衡量标准。
科研与公卫意义
这些结果通过确定覆盖范围的差异以及更好的数据收集方法的需求,为改进免疫规划的策略提供了信息。他们还深入了解破伤风类毒素 疫苗接种 在保护新生儿方面的有效性。
局限与证据强度
该研究的设计依赖于护理人员的回忆,这可能会带来偏见和不准确。证据是中等的,因为调查使用了行政数据并根据退出率进行了调整,但对自我报告数据的依赖和回忆准确性的潜在变化限制了结果的可信度。

2012 Ethiopian national immunization coverage survey

Authors: Coverage Survey Task Force, Habtamu Teklie, Theodros Getachew, James McQuen Patterson, Gavin Grant, François Gasse, Dale Rhoda
Original abstract (English)
Executive summary The Expanded Program for Immunization (EPI) in Ethiopia, launched in 1980, has shown steady progress in increasing coverage for all antigens. Additional vaccines beyond the traditional ones, Hib and Hep‐B in 2007 and PCV in 2011 included in the routine immunization program. Performance report in 2010/11 showed wide variation as reported through HMIS compared to DHS and other assessments. Administrative coverage (through HMIS) was reported as at 87% for DPT‐HepB‐Hib3 in 2010, the Demographic and Health Survey (DHS) estimating DPT‐HepB‐Hib3 (CSA, 2011) to be 36.5% for 2010. In order for meaningful and ongoing program decision‐making, immunization coverage estimates are essential. Such estimates provide data on the success of immunization programs to reach every child and every pregnant woman and also give direction to program experts regarding how to improve programs. Given that coverage surveys should be completed every three to five years (the last was done in 2007), the Federal Minister of Health (FMOH) decided to validate the administrative reports through an exclusive Immunization coverage survey of high quality in 2012. The Interagency Coordination Committee (ICC) organized a task force consisting of representatives from the Ethiopian Health and Nutrition Institute (EHNRI), the Central Statistical Agency (CSA), the Federal Ministry of Health, UNICEF, WHO, Integrated Family Health Partnership, Core Group, The Bill and Melinda Gates Foundation (BMGF) and the Clinton Health Access Initiative. EHNRI administered the survey. The overall objective of the survey was to determine the coverage of all antigens in children 12 to 23 months old and the proportion of children protected at birth from tetanus born to mothers 0 to 11 months prior to the survey at national and regional levels. Methodology The 2012 Immunization Coverage survey was a multi‐stage stratified cluster design cross‐sectional national survey. The survey sample frame was designed to provide estimates for the maternal and infant immunization status at the national level and for each of the nine regions and two city‐administrations. The number of clusters was selected based upon an estimate of the number of eligible children in each cluster and resource availability. A cluster sample size of seven households (HH) for each of the two groups (tetanus immunization and infant immunization groups) was selected after review of inter‐ and intra‐cluster variability in the DHS 2011 survey. These data indicate that seven complete responses per cluster should yield immunization coverage estimates with 95% confidence intervals (CI) that are ±10% precision, based on a coverage estimate of 50% for most regions. At the national level, the precision was designed to be ±5%. In addition to HH surveys, a health facility staff questionnaire was administered at health centers in urban and rural clusters and at health posts only in rural clusters in the nearest functional government health facility for each surveyed cluster. A total of 550 clusters were selected nationally, 50 from each region and in each cluster, 14 households were selected, seven houses with children 12‐23 months and seven houses with infants, 0‐11 months representing recently pregnant mothers. Nineteen clusters were replaced. Of the 3,837 children interviewed and data entered, after cleaning a total of 3,762 caregivers of eligible children from 550 clusters of 11 regions aged 12 ‐ 23 months were analyzed for childhood immunization. A total of 3,843 mothers of children 0‐11 months were interviewed from 550 clusters of 11 regions and analyzed for analysis of maternal tetanus immunization; that is 99.8% of the planned sample. Routine immunization coverage of children 12-­23 months of age For this survey, immunization coverage is defined as reported immunization, evidenced by card, by history from mother/caregiver, or by EPI register from a nearby health facility. Information on child immunization was obtained from 3,762 children aged 12-23 months, of whom 1,756 (46.7%) had immunization cards; and card availability by region ranges from 14.1% in Afar to 94.2% in Addis Ababa. Due to the relatively low‐level of card availability, concerns have been raised about the completeness and accuracy of data collected via caregiver’s recall. The drop‐out rates observed in the data when caregiver recall is the source of information is often twice as high as that seen for cards and EPI register. This analysis adjusts the DPT‐HepB‐Hib‐3 coverage using the DPT‐HepB‐Hib‐1‐3 drop‐out rate from card as a better estimate of the true drop‐out rate for data collected by caregiver recall. The weighted Ethiopian EPI coverage by antigen is BCG 79.6%; DPT‐HepB‐Hib1 80.0%; OPV1 90.1%; adjusted DPT‐ HepB‐Hib3 65.7%; OPV3 70.5%; and measles 68.2%.Coverage for all antigens tends to be higher in children of caregivers with higher educational attainment, higher wealth, children of first parity, and those residing in urban areas. This survey revealed high drop‐out rates in the immunization program. The total unadjusted drop‐out rate (card, verification and history) for DPT‐HepB‐Hib1‐3 was 25.6% nationally, ranging from 2.6% (Addis Ababa) to 63.8% (Somali). Tetanus toxoid coverage and among mothers of children 0­11 months of age Nationwide the proportion of women who delivered in the last year who received at least one dose of TT is 79.9% and who received a second dose is 72.0% conferring immunity for three years. The coverage of TT doses increases among older women and those with higher child parity, high educational attainment, and those living in urban areas. On the other hand, women below 20 years with primipara and less than two ANC visits have lower coverage. National coverage for TT1 vaccine administered during the last pregnancy was 58.7% and for two or more was 44.8%. According to the definition of neonatal protection at birth used in Ethiopia, the percentage of children protected was 65%. Based on the WHO definition for neonatal tetanus protection at birth, the results revealed that nationally 68% of children born are protected. These figures also vary across the regions ranging from 84% in Addis Ababa to 35% Afar regional state. Attended delivery is one the most important indicators of maternal health care services and prevention of neonatal tetanus. According to the findings of this survey deliveries attended by skilled health professional is, by far, lower than attendance by unskilled professionals and unattended deliveries. Characteristics of health facility serving assessed areas Health facilities (health posts and health centers) and health workers are the backbone of immunization service delivery, ensuring that national immunization policy is translated into an effective and usable service for women and children. In total 585 government‐run health facilities assessed during the Ethiopian EPI Coverage Survey (2012); from this, 298 were health centers and 273 were health posts. The survey showed that 97.1% of the urban surveyed health facilities and 89.2% of rural health facilities provide routine immunization services on a regularly basis. However, routine EPI micro‐plans that show the annual/monthly target number of children and the static and outreach/mobile sessions scheduled were available in just 78.4% of facilities providing immunization services. Of assessed health centers, 51.4% provide daily sessions compared to health posts, which tended to provide immunization services on a monthly basis (75.7%). Though not cross checked from the records and the reporting formats, 42.5% of a health facilities had a planned session interrupted in the last 9 months. The success of immunization depends on reliable provision of commodities through the supply chain and availability for use when and where needed in the correct quantities and at the right time. The results of the survey revealed that 92.1% and 77.9% of health posts and health centers, respectively, received the vaccine at least once in a month. With regard to the cold chain, 45.2% of health posts and 2.1% of health centers, reported absence of a vaccine refrigerator. It is also reported that in health posts with refrigerators, 36.6% of refrigerators were not functioning due to shortage of kerosene while 33.0% of health centers reported non‐functional refrigerators due to a broken part; “refrigerator is not installed” accounts 12.5% of unused refrigerators. In the health facilities assessed that store vaccines overnight, 38.6% of health posts and 43.6% of health center+ experienced stock‐outs for one or more of the antigens during previous 3 months before the survey. Recommendations High immunization drop‐out rates could be improved by identifying and addressing the reasons for high drop‐out.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to assess immunization coverage for all antigens in children aged 12 to 23 months and the proportion of children protected at birth from tetanus, as accurate coverage estimates are essential for program decision-making and improving immunization efforts.
Design and population
The 2012 Immunization Coverage survey was a multi-stage stratified cluster design cross-sectional national survey conducted in 550 clusters across 11 regions, targeting children aged 12-23 months and mothers of children 0-11 months. The survey included both household interviews and health facility staff questionnaires.
Core methods
The survey used a cluster sampling method, selecting seven households for each of the two groups (tetanus immunization and infant immunization groups) based on inter- and intra-cluster variability. Immunization coverage was defined as reported immunization through cards, caregiver history, or EPI registers, and data were adjusted using drop-out rates from immunization cards to estimate true coverage.
Main results
The weighted EPI coverage by antigen was BCG 79.6%, DPT-HepB-Hib1 80.0%, OPV1 90.1%, adjusted DPT-HepB-Hib3 65.7%, OPV3 70.5%, and measles 68.2%. The unadjusted drop-out rate for DPT-HepB-Hib1-3 was 25.6% nationally, ranging from 2.6% in Addis Ababa to 63.8% in Somali. Based on the WHO definition, 68% of children born were protected from neonatal tetanus.
Interpretation and novelty
The findings highlight significant drop-out rates in the immunization program and suggest that data collected through caregiver recall may be less accurate than data from immunization cards or EPI registers. The adjusted coverage estimates provide a more reliable measure of immunization success.
Scientific and public-health significance
These results inform strategies to improve immunization programs by identifying disparities in coverage and the need for better data collection methods. They also provide insights into the effectiveness of tetanus toxoid vaccination in protecting newborns.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study's design relied on caregiver recall, which may introduce bias and inaccuracy. The evidence is moderate, as the survey used administrative data and adjusted for drop-out rates, but the reliance on self-reported data and potential variability in recall accuracy limits the confidence in the results.

患病率在麻疹感染医护人员中的作用:一项系统综述和元分析

The prevalence of measles vaccination in measles-infected healthcare workers: A systematic review and meta-analysis
作者： Sumanta Saha
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述重点关注 麻疹病毒(人麻疹病毒)、历史分类学名称、风疹和 麻疹 疾病。核心问题是有记录的接受两剂含有 麻疹 的 麻疹 (MCV) 是否足以证明医护人员 (HCW) 具有 麻疹 免疫力。
证据基础与方法
该综述对 PubMed、Embase 和 Scopus 数据库中自 1974 年以来符合条件的出版物进行了系统检索。汇总的 患病率 使用 患病率 荟萃分析进行估计,并根据自 MCV 纳入研究国家国家免疫计划 (NIS) 以来相对于 麻疹 暴发 年的持续时间进行荟萃回归。
共识与结论
综述发现,在麻疹感染的医护人员中,≥2剂MCV接种的患病率与MCV纳入国家免疫计划(NIS)以来的时间相关,且随着时间推移,麻疹风险增加。然而,由于≥2剂MCV接种或未接种的比例较大且相近,仅凭记录的两剂MCV接种作为证明麻疹免疫力的作用仍不确定。
争议与缺口
该综述强调了 ≥2 剂 MCV 接受者 HCW 中 麻疹 风险的异质性,自 MCV 纳入 NIS 以来,这种异质性随着持续时间的增加而增加。关于记录的 2 剂 MCV 收据作为免疫力证明的充分性尚不明确,并且证据并不能完全解决这种不确定性。
科研与实践启示
该审查表明需要进一步研究来澄清记录的 2 剂 MCV 收据作为 麻疹 免疫力证明的作用。它还意味着监测 麻疹 暴发 以及 疫苗接种 时间表在医护人员中的有效性的重要性。

The prevalence of measles vaccination in measles-infected healthcare workers: A systematic review and meta-analysis

Authors: Sumanta Saha
Original abstract (English)
AIMS: Documentable evidence of previous receipt of 2-dose of measles-containing vaccine (MCV) is often sufficient as proof of measles immunity in healthcare workers (HCWs). The usefulness of this policy was assessed by estimating the global prevalences of different MCV-receipt statuses in measles infected HCWs. METHODS: Eligible publications since 1974 were searched in PubMed, Embase, and Scopus databases. Pooled prevalences were estimated by prevalence meta-analysis and meta-regressed using durations since MCV inclusion in the national immunization schedule (NIS) of respective study countries to measles outbreak years as a moderator. RESULTS: Of the 7571 citations retrieved, 27 studies reporting on 1319 measles-infected HCWs aged 18-60 years (outbreak years: 1985-2019) were reviewed. Prevalences of measles-infected HCWs were- a. by gender: females: 65.0% (95% CI: 57.0, 72.0); males: 35.0% (95% CI: 28.0, 43.0); c. by birth year: born after MCV inclusion in NIS: 77.0% (95% CI: 66.0, 87.0) and in born before: 6.0% (95% CI: 0.0, 17.0); d. by health profession: nurses: 31.0% (95% CI: 24.0, 39.0); doctors: 27.0% (95% CI: 19.0, 36.0); students 5.0% (95% CI: 1.0, 12.0). The measles risk in ≥2-dose of MCV recipient HCWs increased with duration since MCV inclusion in NIS to outbreak occurrences (p < .05). CONCLUSION: In measles infected HCWs, as the rates of being ≥2-dose MCV recipient or unvaccinated were substantial and comparable, the role of documented 2-dose of MCV receipt alone as a proof of measles immunity remains uncertain.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on measles virus (Morbillivirus hominis), historical taxonomic names, rubeola, and measles disease. The central question is whether documented receipt of two doses of measles-containing vaccine (MCV) is sufficient to prove measles immunity in healthcare workers (HCWs).
Evidence base and method
The review conducted a systematic search of PubMed, Embase, and Scopus databases for eligible publications since 1974. Pooled prevalences were estimated using prevalence meta-analysis and meta-regressed based on the duration since MCV inclusion in the national immunization schedule (NIS) of study countries relative to measles outbreak years.
Consensus and conclusions
The review found that among measles-infected HCWs, the prevalence of being a ≥2-dose MCV recipient was associated with increased measles risk over time since MCV inclusion in the NIS. However, the role of documented 2-dose MCV receipt alone as proof of measles immunity remains uncertain due to substantial and comparable rates of being ≥2-dose MCV recipients or unvaccinated.
Controversies and gaps
The review highlights heterogeneity in measles risk among ≥2-dose MCV recipient HCWs, which increased with duration since MCV inclusion in the NIS. There is a lack of clarity regarding the sufficiency of documented 2-dose MCV receipt as proof of immunity, and the evidence does not fully resolve this uncertainty.
Research and practice implications
The review suggests the need for further research to clarify the role of documented 2-dose MCV receipt as proof of measles immunity. It also implies the importance of monitoring measles outbreaks and the effectiveness of vaccination schedules in healthcare workers.

麻疹 前线防御:急诊护士和急救医疗服务人员的关键作用

Measles Frontline Defense: The Pivotal Role of Emergency Nurses and Emergency Medical Services Personnel
作者： Garrett D. Hall, Diana J. Felton, Nathan R. Tan, Sarah K. Kemble
摘要中文翻译简介:麻疹 于 2000 年在美国宣布消除。然而,2025 年,该国报告的病例数达到了 20 多年来的最高水平。此外,疾病控制与预防中心 2026 年初的数据表明病例数持续增加。急诊护士和急诊医疗技术人员经常作为最初的医疗保健专业人员来评估发烧和皮疹患者。及时识别和迅速隔离对于遏制 麻疹 传播 至关重要。这篇叙述性文献综述旨在为急诊护士和急诊医疗技术人员在 麻疹 暴发 期间的识别、隔离、记录、感染控制以及与公共卫生当局的协调方面提供实用的、基于证据的指导。方法:进行了综合当前疾病控制与预防中心和世界卫生组织指南的叙述性回顾。通过使用 麻疹、急诊科、院前、急诊护士、紧急医疗服务、分诊和感染控制等关键词在 PubMed 和 Google Scholar 中进行有针对性的搜索来确定同行评审文献,重点关注 2015 年至 2026 年的出版物。优先考虑提供与紧急医疗环境相关的实用指导或流行病学数据的研究。结果:麻疹 通过空气中的 传播 传播,并在感染者离开某个区域后可在空气中悬浮长达 2 小时。早期症状包括发烧、咳嗽、鼻炎(流鼻涕和鼻塞)和结膜炎,随后出现下降的斑丘疹(皮肤上有平坦区域和小肿块的红色皮疹),通常从面部开始并向下蔓延。适当的分诊评估、感染控制措施、电子健康记录中的记录以及与感染预防和公共卫生当局的及时沟通对于中断 传播 至关重要。讨论:麻疹 仍然是医疗保健环境中最具传染性的病毒性疾病之一。急诊护士和急救医疗技术人员往往是最先识别并阻止 麻疹 传播的人。应采用明确的分类和筛查步骤,应确认工作人员的免疫力,并与感染预防和公共卫生团队密切合作,以保护患者、家庭和医护人员。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
范围:麻疹病毒 (A1) 引起 麻疹 疾病,影响急诊护士和急诊医疗技术人员 (A4),核心问题是如何在紧急情况下识别、隔离、记录和控制 麻疹 传播 (A5、A6)。
证据基础与方法
证据基础:综合 CDC 和 WHO 指南的叙述性审查 (A7),在 PubMed 和 Google Scholar 中搜索 2015-2026 年有关紧急情况下 麻疹 的文献 (A8),并优先考虑实用指南和流行病学数据 (A9)。
共识与结论
共识:麻疹具有高度传染性且为空气传播(A10);早期症状包括发热、咳嗽、鼻炎、结膜炎和皮疹(A11);及时分诊、隔离、感染控制、记录、沟通、工作人员免疫力验证及公共卫生协作是中断传播的关键(A12、A13、A14、A15)。
争议与缺口
争议与缺口:在消除该疾病二十年后再次出现(A2、A3),以及关于最佳急诊分诊和监测方案的数据有限(A3、A15),表明标准化响应框架中的具体证据缺口。
科研与实践启示
启示:实施明确的分诊和筛查步骤,验证工作人员免疫力,强制执行感染控制措施,在电子健康记录中记录,并与感染预防和公共卫生当局保持及时沟通,以保护患者和工作人员(A6、A12、A15)。

Measles Frontline Defense: The Pivotal Role of Emergency Nurses and Emergency Medical Services Personnel

Authors: Garrett D. Hall, Diana J. Felton, Nathan R. Tan, Sarah K. Kemble
Original abstract (English)
INTRODUCTION: Measles was declared eliminated in the United States in 2000. However, in 2025, the country experienced the highest number of cases reported in more than 2 decades. Furthermore, early 2026 data from the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention indicate a continued increase in case counts. Emergency nurses and emergency medical technicians frequently serve as the initial health care professionals who assess patients with fever and rashes. Prompt identification and swift isolation are essential to curtail measles transmission. This narrative literature review aims to provide practical, evidence-based guidance for emergency nurses and emergency medical technicians on the recognition, isolation, documentation, infection control, and coordination with public health authorities during measles outbreaks. METHODS: A narrative review synthesizing current Centers for Disease Control and Prevention and World Health Organization guidance was conducted. Peer-reviewed literature was identified through targeted searches in PubMed and Google Scholar using keywords, including measles, emergency department, prehospital, emergency nurse, emergency medical services, triage, and infection control, focusing on publications from 2015 to 2026. Studies providing practical guidance or epidemiologic data relevant to emergency medical settings were prioritized. RESULTS: Measles spreads through airborne transmission and can remain suspended in the air for up to 2 hours after an infected individual leaves an area. Early symptoms include fever, cough, coryza (runny nose and nasal congestion), and conjunctivitis, followed by a descending maculopapular rash (red rash with both flat areas and small bumps on the skin), which typically begins on the face and spreads downward. Proper triage assessment, infection-control measures, documentation within electronic health records, and timely communication with infection prevention and public health authorities are essential to interrupt the transmission. DISCUSSION: Measles remains one of the most contagious viral illnesses encountered in health care settings. Emergency nurses and emergency medical technicians are often the first to recognize and halt the spread of measles. Clear triage and screening steps should be used, staff immunity should be confirmed, and close collaboration with infection prevention and public health teams is necessary to protect patients, families, and health care workers.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
Scope: Measles virus (A1) causing measles disease, affecting emergency nurses and emergency medical technicians (A4) with the central question of how to recognize, isolate, document, and control measles transmission in emergency settings (A5, A6).
Evidence base and method
Evidence base: Narrative review synthesizing CDC and WHO guidance (A7), searching PubMed and Google Scholar for 2015‐2026 literature on measles in emergency settings (A8), and prioritizing practical guidance and epidemiologic data (A9).
Consensus and conclusions
Consensus: Measles is highly contagious and airborne (A10); early symptoms include fever, cough, coryza, conjunctivitis, and rash (A11); prompt triage, isolation, infection‐control, documentation, communication, staff immunity verification, and public‐health collaboration are essential to interrupt transmission (A12, A13, A14, A15).
Controversies and gaps
Controversies and gaps: The resurgence of measles after two decades of elimination (A2, A3) and limited data on optimal emergency triage and surveillance protocols (A3, A15) indicate concrete evidence gaps in standardized response frameworks.
Research and practice implications
Implications: Implement clear triage and screening steps, verify staff immunity, enforce infection‐control measures, document in electronic health records, and maintain timely communication with infection‐prevention and public‐health authorities to protect patients and staff (A6, A12, A15).

A 高优先级学术文献 · 综述A high priorityAcademic literature · Review

麻疹 麻疹 增强剂可降低免疫功能低下成人的发病率和 死亡率 急性 麻疹 感染:范围界定审查

Measles Vaccine Boosters in Immunocompromised Adults to Reduce Morbidity and Mortality From Acute Measles Infection: A Scoping Review
作者： Isaac Price, Perry K. Smith, Abigail Tan, Annieliesa C Suarez-Rocha, Mariya Baig, Zaida Athouriste, Carole Aziz, Nahinoon Razzaque, Sarah Sadik, Sebastian Breeland
摘要中文翻译尽管存在有效的 疫苗,但 麻疹 是一种与全球重大 死亡率 相关的高度传染性病毒性疾病。随着 COVID-19 大流行后,疫苗 的犹豫不断增加,加上缺乏 麻疹 特异性 抗病毒药物 药物,麻疹 和流行病的风险仍然存在免疫功能低下的个体因 麻疹 出现严重并发症的风险较高,特别是因为严重免疫功能低下的患者可能禁忌使用减毒活疫苗 麻疹、腮腺炎和风疹 疫苗。因此,这些个体依赖群体免疫。本次范围界定审查旨在确定免疫功能低下患者对 麻疹 加强剂量的潜在需求通过提供当前 疫苗 适应症和结果的概述,对美国成人进行了搜索。使用特定于 麻疹 加强剂量和免疫功能低下成人的搜索词对 EMBASE、Web of Science 和 Ovid MEDLINE 进行了搜索。Rayyan 总共导入了 2,335 篇文章,经过两级审查后,有 7 篇文章符合完全纳入标准。结果显示,患有慢性免疫功能低下疾病的个体往往没有得到充分的保护麻疹,使他们容易受到感染。本次综述的结果表明,临床医生可以考虑对免疫功能低下个体进行 麻疹 加强剂量,以帮助他们恢复对 麻疹 的保护。同时,根据这些发现,有必要利用更大的样本量和不同的患者群体进行进一步的研究。我们认为,其他研究应该旨在澄清当前的指南,以改善可能接触 麻疹 的免疫功能低下患者的护理麻疹。

证据基础与方法

Measles Vaccine Boosters in Immunocompromised Adults to Reduce Morbidity and Mortality From Acute Measles Infection: A Scoping Review

Authors: Isaac Price, Perry K. Smith, Abigail Tan, Annieliesa C Suarez-Rocha, Mariya Baig, Zaida Athouriste, Carole Aziz, Nahinoon Razzaque, Sarah Sadik, Sebastian Breeland
Original abstract (English)
Measles is a highly contagious viral illness associated with significant global mortality, despite the availability of an effective vaccine.As vaccine hesitancy continues to increase following the COVID-19 pandemic, combined with a lack of measles-specific antiviral agents, the risk of outbreaks and epidemics remains elevated.Immunocompromised individuals have a higher risk of serious complications from measles, especially because the live-attenuated measles, mumps, and rubella vaccine may be contraindicated in severely immunocompromised patients.As such, these individuals depend on herd immunity.This scoping review aims to identify the potential need for a measles booster dose among immunocompromised adults in the United States by providing an overview of current vaccine indications and outcomes.EMBASE, Web of Science, and Ovid MEDLINE were searched using search terms specific to measles booster doses and immunocompromised adults.In total, 2,335 articles were imported to Rayyan, and after a two-tiered review, seven articles met the full inclusion criteria.The results revealed that individuals with chronic immunocompromising illnesses were often not fully protected against measles, leaving them vulnerable to infection.The findings from this review suggest that clinicians may consider a booster dose of vaccine for immunocompromised individuals to help restore their protection against measles.Meanwhile, further research utilizing larger sample sizes and diverse patient populations is warranted based on these findings.We believe that additional studies should aim to clarify current guidelines to improve the care of immunocompromised patients who may be exposed to measles.

Scope and question

Evidence base and method
Measles is a highly contagious viral illness associated with significant global mortality, despite the availability of an effective vaccine.As vaccine hesitancy continues to increase following the COVID-19 pandemic, combined with a lack of measles-specific antiviral agents, the risk of outbreaks and epidemics remains elevated.Immunocompromised individuals have a higher risk of serious complications from measles, especially because the live-attenuated measles, mumps, and rubella vaccine may be contraindicated in severely immunocompromised patients.As such, these individuals depend on herd immunity.This scoping review aims to identify the potential need for a measles booster dose among immunocompromised adults in the United States by providing an overview of current vaccine indications and outcomes.EMBASE, Web of Science, and Ovid MEDLINE were searched using search terms specific to measles booster doses and immunocompromised adults.In total, 2,335 articles were imported to Rayyan, and after a two-tiered review, seven articles met the full inclusion criteria.The results revealed that individuals with chronic immunocompromising illnesses were often not fully protected against measles, leaving them vulnerable to infection.The findings from this review suggest that clinicians may consider a booster dose of vaccine for immunocompromised individuals to help restore their protection against measles.Meanwhile, further research utilizing larger sample sizes and diverse patient populations is warranted based on these findings.We believe that additional studies should aim to clarify current guidelines to improve the care of immunocompromised patients who may be exposed to measles.

常规含麻疹的疫苗接种接种后儿童1–5岁群体的血清学保护:一项系统综述

Serological Protection After Routine Measles-Containing Vaccination Among Children Aged 1–5 Years in Low- and Middle-Income Countries: A Systematic Review
作者： Imtiaz Hussain, Babar Tasneem Shaikh, Haider Abbas, Ahmad Khan, Sajid Bashir Soofi, Haider Abbass
摘要中文翻译背景:尽管在低收入和中等收入国家(LMICs)广泛实施了常规两剂含麻疹的疫苗(MCV)接种计划,麻疹仍然是导致儿童发病率和死亡率的主要原因。血清学评估提供了对疫苗诱导免疫的客观衡量;然而,关于LMICs中儿童完成两剂疫苗接种后血清学保护的证据仍较为零散且方法学不一致。目标:综合分析LMICs中1–5岁儿童常规两剂MCV接种后血清学保护的证据,并识别与免疫接种计划相关的免疫决定因素、方法学局限性和证据缺口。方法:本系统综述遵循首选报告项目清单(PRISMA 2020)指南,并在PROSPERO(CRD420261300545)前瞻性注册。文献检索于2026年1月21日在PubMed、MEDLINE、Scopus、Cochrane Library、CINAHL(EBSCO)和SciSpace数据库中进行。两名评审员独立筛选研究、提取数据并使用Joanna Briggs Institute的批判性评价工具评估证据质量。由于临床和方法学上的显著异质性,无法进行元分析;因此,结果以叙述性方式综合。结果:共有18项研究符合纳入标准。这些研究来自四个世界卫生组织地区中的14个LMICs(61.1%来自非洲地区,27.8%来自东地中海地区,南亚和美洲地区各占5.6%)。完成两剂接种计划后,报告的MCV血清保护率通常在30.6%至100%之间,最高水平为87–100%,但在某些特定环境中,如农村巴基斯坦(30.6%)和埃塞俄比亚(40–54%),尽管有记录的疫苗接种接种,仍显示出明显的较低保护水平。较低的保护水平与营养不良、人类免疫缺陷病毒(HIV)感染、不完整或延迟的疫苗接种接种、首次接种年龄较小以及免疫接种计划执行不力等因素密切相关。研究设计、疫苗接种接种计划、实验室检测方法(如ELISA、PRNT、凝集抑制试验等)、血清保护阈值、抗体检测时间点以及受试者特征等方面存在显著差异。偏倚风险总体为中等至高,证据确定性从低到非常低不等。结论:常规两剂含麻疹的疫苗接种接种通常在LMICs中1–5岁儿童中提供显著的血清学保护。然而,方法学异质性和地理证据缺口限制了直接比较,并降低了跨研究解释的置信度。需要更大的血清学评估标准化、统一的报告方法以及设计良好的纵向研究,以加强证据基础并支持旨在实现和维持麻疹消除的免疫接种政策。

范围与问题
尽管在低收入和中等收入国家(LMICs)广泛实施了常规两剂含麻疹的疫苗(MCV)接种计划,麻疹仍然是导致儿童发病率和死亡率的主要原因。目标是综合分析LMICs中1–5岁儿童常规两剂MCV接种后血清学保护的证据,并识别与免疫接种计划相关的免疫决定因素、方法学局限性和证据缺口。
证据基础与方法
本系统综述遵循首选报告项目清单(PRISMA 2020)指南,并在PROSPERO(CRD420261300545)前瞻性注册。文献检索于2026年1月21日在PubMed、MEDLINE、Scopus、Cochrane Library、CINAHL(EBSCO)和SciSpace数据库中进行。两名评审员独立筛选研究、提取数据并使用Joanna Briggs Institute的批判性评价工具评估证据质量。由于临床和方法学上的显著异质性,无法进行元分析;因此,结果以叙述性方式综合。
共识与结论
常规两剂含麻疹的疫苗接种接种通常在LMICs中1–5岁儿童中提供显著的血清学保护。完成两剂接种计划后,报告的MCV血清保护率通常在30.6%至100%之间,最高水平为87–100%,但在某些特定环境中,如农村巴基斯坦(30.6%)和埃塞俄比亚(40–54%),尽管有记录的疫苗接种接种,仍显示出明显的较低保护水平。
争议与缺口
研究设计、疫苗接种接种计划、实验室检测方法(如ELISA、PRNT、凝集抑制试验等)、血清保护阈值、抗体检测时间点以及受试者特征等方面存在显著差异。偏倚风险总体为中等至高,证据确定性从低到非常低不等。
科研与实践启示
较低的保护始终与营养不良、人类免疫缺陷病毒 (HIV) 感染、疫苗接种 不完整或延迟、首次剂量较年轻以及计划表现较差有关。需要提高血清学评估和阈值的标准化、统一的报告方法以及精心设计的纵向研究,以加强证据基础并支持旨在实现和维持 麻疹 消除的免疫政策。

Serological Protection After Routine Measles-Containing Vaccination Among Children Aged 1–5 Years in Low- and Middle-Income Countries: A Systematic Review

Authors: Imtiaz Hussain, Babar Tasneem Shaikh, Haider Abbas, Ahmad Khan, Sajid Bashir Soofi, Haider Abbass
Original abstract (English)
Background: Measles remains a major cause of morbidity and mortality among children in low- and middle-income countries (LMICs), despite widespread implementation of routine two-dose measles-containing vaccine (MCV) schedules. Serological assessment provides an objective measure of vaccine-induced immunity; however, evidence describing post-vaccination serological protection among children in LMICs remains fragmented and methodologically heterogeneous. Objective: To synthesize evidence on serological protection following routine two-dose MCV among children aged 1–5 years in LMICs and to identify determinants of immunity, methodological limitations, and evidence gaps relevant to immunization programmes. Methods: This systematic review was conducted in accordance with the Preferred Reporting Items for Systematic Reviews and Meta-Analyses (PRISMA 2020) guidelines and prospectively registered in PROSPERO (CRD420261300545). Literature searches were conducted in PubMed, MEDLINE, Scopus, Cochrane Library, CINAHL (EBSCO), and SciSpace on 21 January 2026. Two reviewers independently screened studies, extracted data, and assessed quality of evidence using Joanna Briggs Institute critical appraisal tools. Substantial clinical and methodological heterogeneity precluded meta-analysis; therefore, findings were synthesized narratively. Results: Eighteen studies met the eligibility. These studies were from 14 LMICs across four WHO regions (61.1% African Region, 27.8% Eastern Mediterranean Region, 5.6% each South-East Asia and the region of the Americas). Reported MCV seroprotection following completion of the two-dose schedule generally ranged from 30.6% to 100%, with the highest levels (87–100%), but with evident lower protection in specific settings, such as rural Pakistan (30.6%) and Ethiopia (40–54%) despite having documented vaccination records. Lower protection was consistently associated with undernutrition, human immunodeficiency virus (HIV) infection, incomplete or delayed vaccination, younger age at first dose, and weak programme performance. Considerable variability was observed in study design, vaccination schedules, laboratory assays (ELISA, PRNT, haemagglutination inhibition, and others), seroprotective thresholds, timing of antibody assessment, and participant characteristics. Risk of bias was largely moderate-to-high, and certainty of evidence ranged from low to very low. Conclusions: Routine two-dose measles vaccination generally provides substantial serological protection among children aged 1–5 years in LMICs. Nevertheless, methodological heterogeneity and geographical evidence gaps limit direct comparisons and reduce confidence in cross-study interpretation. Greater standardization of serological assessment and thresholds, harmonized reporting methods, and well-designed longitudinal studies are needed to strengthen the evidence base and support immunization policies aimed at achieving and sustaining measles elimination.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
Measles remains a major cause of morbidity and mortality among children in low- and middle-income countries (LMICs), despite widespread implementation of routine two-dose measles-containing vaccine (MCV) schedules. To synthesize evidence on serological protection following routine two-dose MCV among children aged 1–5 years in LMICs and to identify determinants of immunity, methodological limitations, and evidence gaps relevant to immunization programmes.
Evidence base and method
This systematic review was conducted in accordance with the Preferred Reporting Items for Systematic Reviews and Meta-Analyses (PRISMA 2020) guidelines and prospectively registered in PROSPERO (CRD420261300545). Literature searches were conducted in PubMed, MEDLINE, Scopus, Cochrane Library, CINAHL (EBSCO), and SciSpace on 21 January 2026. Two reviewers independently screened studies, extracted data, and assessed quality of evidence using Joanna Briggs Institute critical appraisal tools. Substantial clinical and methodological heterogeneity precluded meta-analysis; therefore, findings were synthesized narratively.
Consensus and conclusions
Routine two-dose measles vaccination generally provides substantial serological protection among children aged 1–5 years in LMICs. Reported MCV seroprotection following completion of the two-dose schedule generally ranged from 30.6% to 100%, with the highest levels (87–100%), but with evident lower protection in specific settings, such as rural Pakistan (30.6%) and Ethiopia (40–54%) despite having documented vaccination records.
Controversies and gaps
Considerable variability was observed in study design, vaccination schedules, laboratory assays (ELISA, PRNT, haemagglutination inhibition, and others), seroprotective thresholds, timing of antibody assessment, and participant characteristics. Risk of bias was largely moderate-to-high, and certainty of evidence ranged from low to very low.
Research and practice implications
Lower protection was consistently associated with undernutrition, human immunodeficiency virus (HIV) infection, incomplete or delayed vaccination, younger age at first dose, and weak programme performance. Greater standardization of serological assessment and thresholds, harmonized reporting methods, and well-designed longitudinal studies are needed to strengthen the evidence base and support immunization policies aimed at achieving and sustaining measles elimination.

自身免疫性风湿性疾病中的疫苗接种考虑因素

Vaccination considerations in autoimmune rheumatic diseases.
作者： Meliha C Kapetanovic
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本综述聚焦于自身免疫性风湿性疾病(ARDs)中的疫苗接种考虑因素,具体探讨ARDs患者中疫苗可预防感染的风险、免疫抑制治疗对疫苗应答的影响,以及优化疫苗接种策略的重要性。
证据基础与方法
本综述是对现有文献的叙述性综合,没有明确提及系统搜索方法、数据库或荟萃分析。它包含文献中的一般观察和启示,但未详细说明纳入标准或研究选择过程。
共识与结论
综述得出结论,自身免疫性风湿性疾病(ARDs)患者由于免疫失调、免疫抑制治疗和合并症,面临疫苗可预防感染的更高风险。它强调了优化疫苗接种时机与免疫抑制治疗之间关系的重要性,并指出提高疫苗接种率有助于减轻感染负担。
争议与缺口
该综述指出,关于长期 疫苗 诱导的免疫力、加强剂量的需求以及 疫苗 对 ARD 患者的真实有效性的证据仍然有限。此外,虽然没有详细说明,但强调了道德考虑。
科研与实践启示
该综述表明,提高 ARD 患者对 疫苗 的吸收可以减轻感染负担,有助于群体免疫,并为其他弱势群体提供间接保护。它还强调了进一步研究 疫苗 的耐久性和有效性的重要性。

Vaccination considerations in autoimmune rheumatic diseases.

Authors: Meliha C Kapetanovic
Original abstract (English)
Patients with autoimmune rheumatic diseases (ARDs) are at increased risk of numerous vaccine-preventable infections owing to a combination of disease-related immune dysregulation, immunosuppressive therapies - including glucocorticoids - and comorbidities. Age-related immunosenescence further increases this susceptibility in older patients. The SARS-CoV-2 (COVID-19) pandemic and measles outbreaks have underscored the importance of optimizing vaccination strategies and improving adherence to recommendations. Nevertheless, coverage for routinely recommended vaccines, including influenza and pneumococcal vaccines, remains suboptimal in this population. Vaccine hesitancy is an important barrier driven by concerns regarding safety, tolerability, disease flares and the potential induction of new autoimmune phenomena. Advanced therapies - including B cell-depleting agents, chimeric antigen receptor T cell therapy and bispecific T cell engagers - can substantially impair vaccine-induced immune responses, highlighting the importance of optimizing vaccination timing in relation to treatment. Although most studies focus on humoral and cellular immune responses, evidence on the long-term durability of vaccine-induced immunity, the need for booster doses and real-world vaccine effectiveness in patients with ARDs remains limited. Ethical considerations are important. Although vaccination in adults is generally not mandatory, improving vaccine uptake reduces the burden of infections in patients with ARDs, contributes to herd immunity and provides indirect protection for other vulnerable populations.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on vaccination considerations in autoimmune rheumatic diseases (ARDs), specifically examining the risk of vaccine-preventable infections in patients with ARDs, the impact of immunosuppressive therapies on vaccine responses, and the importance of optimizing vaccination strategies.
Evidence base and method
The review is a narrative synthesis of existing literature, with no explicit mention of systematic search methods, databases, or meta-analysis. It includes general observations and implications from the literature without detailed eligibility criteria or study selection processes.
Consensus and conclusions
The review concludes that patients with ARDs are at increased risk of vaccine-preventable infections due to immune dysregulation, immunosuppressive therapies, and comorbidities. It highlights the need for optimizing vaccination timing relative to treatment and improving vaccine uptake to reduce infection burden.
Controversies and gaps
The review notes that evidence on long-term vaccine-induced immunity, the need for booster doses, and real-world vaccine effectiveness in patients with ARDs remains limited. Additionally, ethical considerations are emphasized, though not elaborated.
Research and practice implications
The review suggests that improving vaccine uptake in patients with ARDs can reduce infection burden, contribute to herd immunity, and provide indirect protection for other vulnerable populations. It also underscores the importance of further research on vaccine durability and effectiveness in this group.

从标记与重新捕捉视角审视疫苗接种接触点

A mark and recapture perspective on vaccination touchpoints
作者： Niket Thakkar
摘要中文翻译本文将大规模疫苗接种活动视为一种重新捕捉估计,作为常规免疫接种所标记目标人群的估计方法。将该活动视为一种测量手段,我们探讨其特性,包括该活动对目标人群的覆盖率以及活动后覆盖率调查(PCCS)的一些隐含抽样特性,后者是当前实施质量测量的黄金标准。我们以2023年尼日利亚卡诺州麻疹活动为背景,发展这一想法,该活动有疫苗接种团队详细收集的实施数据。具体分析这些团队的统计表,以及他们每日记录的接种对象,我们发现重新捕捉估计与相应PCCS的估计之间存在显著差异。探讨应用于统计表和PCCS的各种偏差模型有助于阐明现场轶事问题如何与这种差异相关联。总体而言,我们发现尽管统计表是一种非常规的人群样本,但它们为实施和测量提供了可行的视角,这种视角原则上可以几乎即时获得,并且对任何疫苗接种接触点都具有高分辨率。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本文将大规模疫苗接种活动,作为全球许多地区疫苗访问的主要平台,视为一种重新捕捉估计,以评估常规免疫接种所标记的目标人群。麻疹病毒(Morbillivirus hominis)的历史分类名称、麻疹(rubeola)和麻疹疾病是研究重点。核心问题是:如何利用标记与重新捕捉框架分析疫苗接种接触点,以评估实施质量。
证据基础与方法
将活动作为一种衡量标准,我们了解其属性,包括活动对目标人群的覆盖范围以及活动后覆盖率调查(PCCS)的一些隐含抽样属性,这是当前实施质量衡量的黄金标准。审查方法涉及分析 疫苗接种 团队在尼日利亚卡诺州参与 2023 年 麻疹 活动所收集的实施数据。
共识与结论
我们在尼日利亚卡诺州 2023 年 麻疹 活动的背景下提出了这一想法,我们拥有参与该活动的 麻疹 团队收集的详细实施数据。总的来说,我们发现,尽管统计表是一个非正统的人口样本,但它提供了一种易于处理的实施和测量视角,原则上几乎可以立即以高分辨率获取任何 疫苗接种 接触点。
争议与缺口
特别查看各队的统计表以及他们接种疫苗的每日记录,我们发现重新捕获估计值与相应 PCCS 的估计值之间存在显着差异。探索应用于统计表和 PCCS 的各种偏差模型有助于澄清现场的轶事问题与这种差异之间的关系。
科研与实践启示
其含义表明,统计表可以提供一种高分辨率、近乎即时的方法来评估实施质量,这可以补充传统的活动后覆盖率调查。

A mark and recapture perspective on vaccination touchpoints

Authors: Niket Thakkar
Original abstract (English)
This paper considers large-scale vaccination campaigns, a major platform for vaccine access in a lot of the world, as a recapture estimate of the target population marked by routine immunization. Framing the campaign as a measurement, we learn about its properties, including the campaign’s coverage of the target population and some implied sampling properties of post-campaign coverage surveys (PCCSs), the current gold-standard in implementation quality measurement. We develop this idea in the context of the 2023 measles campaign in Kano State, Nigeria, where we have detailed implementation data collected by vaccination teams involved in that effort. Looking specifically at the teams’ tally sheets, the daily records of who they vaccinated, we find significant discrepancies between the recapture estimates and those from the corresponding PCCS. Exploring a variety of bias models applied to both the tally sheets and the PCCS helps clarify how anecdotal issues from the field relate to this discrepancy. Overall, we find that the tally sheets, despite being an unorthodox population sample, provide a tractable perspective on implementation and measurement, one that’s in principle available nearly instantly and at high resolution for any vaccination touchpoint.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This paper considers large-scale vaccination campaigns, a major platform for vaccine access in a lot of the world, as a recapture estimate of the target population marked by routine immunization. Measles virus (Morbillivirus hominis), historical taxonomic names, rubeola and measles disease are the focus. The central question is how vaccination touchpoints can be analyzed using a mark and recapture framework to assess implementation quality.
Evidence base and method
Framing the campaign as a measurement, we learn about its properties, including the campaign’s coverage of the target population and some implied sampling properties of post-campaign coverage surveys (PCCSs), the current gold-standard in implementation quality measurement. The review method involves analyzing implementation data collected by vaccination teams involved in the 2023 measles campaign in Kano State, Nigeria.
Consensus and conclusions
We develop this idea in the context of the 2023 measles campaign in Kano State, Nigeria, where we have detailed implementation data collected by vaccination teams involved in that effort. Overall, we find that the tally sheets, despite being an unorthodox population sample, provide a tractable perspective on implementation and measurement, one that’s in principle available nearly instantly and at high resolution for any vaccination touchpoint.
Controversies and gaps
Looking specifically at the teams’ tally sheets, the daily records of who they vaccinated, we find significant discrepancies between the recapture estimates and those from the corresponding PCCS. Exploring a variety of bias models applied to both the tally sheets and the PCCS helps clarify how anecdotal issues from the field relate to this discrepancy.
Research and practice implications
The implications suggest that tally sheets can offer a high-resolution, near-instantaneous method for assessing implementation quality, which may complement traditional post-campaign coverage surveys.

认识并解决孩子父母并不总是直接表达的 疫苗 犹豫

Recognising and Addressing Vaccine Hesitancy That Is Not Always Directly Voiced by a Child's Parents
作者： Alessandro Diana
摘要中文翻译一位母亲带着她九个月大的孩子进行例行检查,一切都很正常。你说“我们今天会给你的孩子做麻疹,腮腺炎,风疹,水痘疫苗”。母亲有两秒没有说话,看着父亲。然后她把孩子的健康记录放进包里,说“我不想现在做。我们会在一两个月后预订一些东西。”她没有拒绝、提出疑虑或提到自闭症、铝或任何你接受过回答训练的问题。听起来像是安排决定,实际上是犹豫。她没有明说就推迟了 疫苗。每个已发布的 疫苗 对话算法都假设她没有说过或做过的事情。所有指导都建议分两步进行。首先是基于父母会同意的假设,发起与父母的对话。第二是使用同理心动机访谈是最广泛采用的形式。2026 年发表的临床实践评论[1]重申,这是共识,并明确了第二步应该何时开始。推荐 疫苗接种,将其作为规范,如果父母表达担忧或拒绝让孩子接种疫苗,则转到第二步。美国儿科学会 2024 年的临床报告,来自同一主要作者,提倡相同的方法方法以及由此衍生的培训计划[2]。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述重点关注 麻疹病毒(人麻疹病毒)、历史分类学名称、风疹和 麻疹 疾病。它解决了孩子父母没有直接表达的 疫苗 犹豫,特别是在 麻疹 疫苗接种 的情况下。核心问题是如何在常规儿科就诊期间识别和解决这种不言而喻的犹豫。
证据基础与方法
证据基础包括 2026 年发表的临床实践综述 [1] 和美国儿科学会 2024 年的临床报告 [2]。这些消息来源概述了 疫苗 对话的两步方法,首先假设父母同意,然后在出现问题时采用同理心沟通。审查方法基于共识,没有明确报告荟萃分析。
共识与结论
共识是,疫苗 的犹豫可能是微妙的,不会直接由父母表达出来。主要结论是,医疗保健提供者应推荐 疫苗接种 作为规范,并且只有在父母表达担忧或拒绝 疫苗 时才进行同理心沟通。这一方法得到了 2026 年临床实践审查和 2024 年美国儿科学会报告的支持。
争议与缺口
该评论强调了在解决不言而喻的犹豫方面的差距,因为现有算法假设父母直接同意。在这种情况下,共情沟通的有效性存在争议,因为证据没有提供不同策略的详细结果或比较数据。
科研与实践启示
审查表明需要进一步研究识别和解决未言明的 疫苗 犹豫。它还意味着对医疗保健提供者进行同理心沟通技巧培训的重要性,以更好地应对抵制或犹豫的父母。此外,它还要求改进 监测 并记录临床环境中的此类犹豫模式。

Recognising and Addressing Vaccine Hesitancy That Is Not Always Directly Voiced by a Child's Parents

Authors: Alessandro Diana
Original abstract (English)
A mother brings her nine-month-old child for a routine visit and everything is normal.You say 'We will give your child the measles, mumps, rubella, chickenpox vaccine today'.The mother says nothing for 2 s and looks at the father.Then she puts the child's health record into her bag and says 'I'd rather not do it now.We'll book something in a month or two.'She has not refused, raised a concern or mentioned autism, aluminium or anything you have been trained to answer.What sounds like a scheduling decision is, in fact, hesitation.She is putting the vaccine off without saying so.Every published algorithm for the vaccine conversation assumes something she has not said or done.All the guidance recommends a two-step sequence.The first is to initiate the conversation with the parents, based on the assumption that they will agree.The second is to use empathic communication with those who resist.Motivational interviewing is the most widely adopted form of this.A clinical practice review published in 2026 [1] restated that this was about consensus and it was explicit about when the second step should begin.Recommend the vaccination, present it as the norm and go to step two if the parents express concerns or decline to have their child vaccinated.The clinical report from the American Academy of Pediatrics in 2024, from the same lead author, promoted the same approach and so has the training programme derived from it [2].
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on measles virus (Morbillivirus hominis), historical taxonomic names, rubeola, and measles disease. It addresses vaccine hesitancy that is not directly voiced by a child's parents, particularly in the context of measles vaccination. The central question is how to identify and address such unspoken hesitancy during routine pediatric visits.
Evidence base and method
The evidence base includes a clinical practice review published in 2026 [1] and a clinical report from the American Academy of Pediatrics in 2024 [2]. These sources outline a two-step approach for vaccine conversations, assuming parental agreement initially and employing empathic communication if concerns arise. The review method is based on consensus and does not explicitly report meta-analysis.
Consensus and conclusions
The consensus is that vaccine hesitancy can be subtle and not directly expressed by parents. The key conclusion is that healthcare providers should recommend vaccination as the norm and only proceed to empathic communication if parents express concerns or decline the vaccine. This approach is supported by both the 2026 clinical practice review and the 2024 American Academy of Pediatrics report.
Controversies and gaps
The review highlights a gap in addressing unspoken hesitancy, as existing algorithms assume direct parental agreement. There is controversy around the effectiveness of empathic communication in such cases, as the evidence does not provide detailed outcomes or comparative data on different strategies.
Research and practice implications
The review suggests a need for further research on identifying and addressing unspoken vaccine hesitancy. It also implies the importance of training healthcare providers in empathic communication techniques to better handle resistant or hesitant parents. Additionally, it calls for improved surveillance and documentation of such hesitancy patterns in clinical settings.

为非洲本地制造配制多剂量冻干 麻疹-风疹 (MR) 疫苗 候选药物的案例研究:实验室规模的结果和为未来技术转让吸取的经验教训

Case Study on Formulating a Multidose Lyophilized Measles-Rubella (MR) Vaccine Candidate for Local Manufacturing in Africa: Lab-Scale Results and Lessons-Learned for Future Technology Transfer
作者： Kathryn Secrist, Prashant Kumar, Michael Z. Wang, Soraia Saleh-Birdjandi, Sagar Salave, Ozan S. Kumru, Ngana Salem Mberkadji, Fama Sakho, Sangeeta B. Joshi, David B. Volkin

Ohio measles cases grow again amid outbreak, cases spike in Wayne County

News Brief

Original source text (English)
Find us on Google 📌 America's birthday 🎂 Start the day smarter ☀️ Get the USA TODAY app U.S. Politics Sports Entertainment Life Money Travel Opinion Crossword ONLY AT USA TODAY: Newsletters For Subscribers Real Estate From the Archives USA TODAY PLAY Crossword Marvel Comics eNewspaper Magazines Investigations Video Podcasts Humankind Advertise Your Business WITNESS (True Crime) Just Curious Best-selling Booklist Top Workplaces Nomination Legals Press Releases Cars Grocery Shopping OUR PORTFOLIO: Real Estate 10BEST USA TODAY Store Shopping Blueprint WITNESS (True Crime) Southern Kitchen Home Internet Services HEALTHCARE Ohio Department of Health Add Topic Ohio measles cases grow again amid outbreak, cases spike in Wayne County Chad Murphy James Powel Mary Walrath-Holdridge USA TODAY NETWORK Sept. 4, 2026, 9:29 a.m. ET Hear this story Nationwide, measles cases have reached a 35-year high, a trend health experts attribute to declining vaccination rates. Health officials state the disease is preventable and strongly encourage vaccination as the best form of protection. All recent measles cases in Wayne County, Ohio, and two recent deaths in Pennsylvania were among unvaccinated individuals. New measles cases in Ohio nearly doubled from week to week as one county reported 5 amid the state's third measles outbreak of 2026. Ohio had 15 new cases in the past week, up from eight the week before. The Ohio Department of Health is reporting 122 cases in 14 counties this year, according to its summary of infectious diseases dashboard, updated Sept. 3. That's up from 107 cases in 12 counties on Aug. 27; 99 cases in 12 counties Aug. 20; 69 cases in 11 counties on Aug. 13; and 45 cases in 10 counties on Aug. 6. The Morgan County Health Department confirmed a case of measles on Sept. 2. The person was likely exposed at Buster Miller Farm in Middlefield on Aug. 14. The Warren City Health Department reported on Aug. 28 that people in the emergency room and its waiting area at Mercy Health St. Joseph Warren Hospital, 667 Eastland Ave., were potentially exposed to measles on Aug. 23. Additionally, the Wayne County Health Department confirmed five new measles cases on Aug. 27, with another 29 probable or suspected. All are among unvaccinated people. “It’s unfortunate that these outbreaks have become more common in Ohio over the past few years, because this disease is preventable,” Dr. Bruce Vanderhoff, ODH director, said a July 30 statement. “This serves to emphasize the importance of being fully vaccinated. I strongly encourage you to protect yourselves and your children by getting vaccinated.” Measles cases nationwide hit a 35-year high for a disease once declared eradicated in the United States. Data released July 22 by the Johns Hopkins University shows 2,295 measles cases have been reported nationwide so far this year, surpassing the 2,286 infections recorded during all of 2025 and marking the highest annual total since 1991. Measles growing in Ohio; counties reporting cases Ohio counties reporting measles cases since Jan. 1, 2026, include: Ashtabula: 27 Cuyahoga: 3 Franklin: 7 Gallia: 1 Geauga: 32 Harrison: 10 Holmes: 6 Knox: 4 Meigs: 4 Morgan: 1 Ottawa: 1 Trumbull: 5 Tuscarawas: 12 Wayne: 9 The case count reflects two previous outbreaks in Cuyahoga County in January and Franklin County in February. The ages of those with confirmed cases range from 15 months to 20 years old, according to various health departments. Measles cases have been growing in Ohio over the past several years, with one case in 2023, seven in 2024 and 45 cases in 2025. Pennsylvania measles deaths At least two people this year have died from measles in Pennsylvania, USA TODAY reports. Both were unvaccinated. The measles deaths were the first in Pennsylvania in 35 years, the state Department of Health said in an Aug. 25 news release, and the first such deaths in the United States this year, according to Centers for Disease Control and Prevention data. Where are measles cases spreading? While Harrison County Public Health reported one case of measles as of Aug. 7, the state dashboard shows two additional cases there. The Wayne County Health Department reports some of its cases are likely associated with a previously identified measles exposure event at Smokey Lane Stables, 686 Smokey Lane Road NW in Sugarcreek, on July 17. One of the Tuscarawas cases is from the same event, that county health department reports. Geauga Public Health reports that the individuals with measles visited several locations while able to spread the virus, including Bloomfield Horse Sale, 2211 Kinsman Road NW, on July 18. The Tuscarawas health department reports that another of its cases was exposed at the Bloomfield Livestock Auction, at the same address as Bloomfield Horse Sale, on on the same day. In Trumbull County, health officials report that infected individuals were at the Walmart in Liberty on July 29 and Akron Children's Mahoning Valley Emergency Department in Boardman on Aug. 2, during the time they were able to spread the virus. Measles cases hit 35-year high in United States The United States declared measles eliminated in 2000 after recording just 86 cases that year, according to USA TODAY, citing the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention. But a series of outbreaks that began in early 2025, centered initially in Gaines County, Texas, has helped drive the virus' resurgence. Health experts say the recent surge has been fueled by declining vaccination rates that have left more communities vulnerable to outbreaks. Why are measles cases on the rise? Ohio vaccination rates Vaccination for measles has declined in recent years, resulting in a growing number of states no longer reporting rates consistent with herd immunity. Herd immunity occurs when more than 95% of people in a community are vaccinated. Most people are then protected, even if they cannot get the vaccine themselves, because those around them are immune and cannot spread the disease. Vaccination coverage among U.S. kindergartners decreased from 95.2% during the 2019–20 school year to 92.5% in the 2024–25 school year, well under the threshold necessary for effective herd immunity, according to the CDC. Declining protections have also been attributed to the growing anti-vaccine movement, which was bolstered by the confirmation of vaccine skeptic Robert F. Kennedy Jr. as health secretary. In addition to making anti-vaccine comments, Kennedy also downplayed pediatric deaths related to measles and touted false cures and preventives, such as the use of vitamin A. During the height of the Texas outbreak, Kennedy encouraged inoculation against measles, pulling back from his previous anti-vaccine messaging. Kennedy is now encouraging the measles vaccine. In Ohio, MMR vaccine — which protects against measles, mumps and rubella — coverage rate for children and adolescents was 91.5% for 2023, the most recent year available from ODH. Previous years ranged from an 80% to 90% immunization rate: 2019: 81.8% 2020: 88.0% 2021: 91.7% 2022: 89.0% Trump signs executive order to separate MMR vaccine President Donald Trump in August 2026 signed an executive order to split the childhood measles, mumps and rubella vaccine into three shots and delivering them at separate medical visits as well as "calling for more vaccine flexibility and research" and fewer childhood vaccinations, USA TODAY reports. "You have the MMR, we want it in three separate vaccinations, given at separate times. Together there could be a possibility they're quite lethal and separately, it looks like they are not at all lethal, but just very effective," said Trump from the Oval Office as he signed the order. But the MMR vaccine is not lethal, and medical research shows no health benefit or reduced risk from splitting it. In contrast, separating the shots increases injection pain, leaves children unprotected longer, and lower overall vaccination rates, according to experts. The MMR vaccine has been in existence since 1971, according to the World Health Organization. Can you get measles if vaccinated? Two doses of MMR are 97% effective against measles, ODH reports. If you are up to date on the measles vaccine, the risk of getting sick is very low. Are measles deadly? Measles can be deadly. ODH states that complications from measles are more common among children younger than 5 years of age, adults older than 20 years of age, pregnant women, and people with compromised immune systems. As many as one out of every 20 children with measles gets pneumonia, the most common cause of death from measles in young children. What does measles look like? Signs, symptoms Symptoms usually appear seven to 14 days after exposure, but it can take up to 21 days. Symptoms include a rash, high fever, runny nose, cough, loss of appetite and red, watery eyes. The rash usually lasts five to six days and begins at the hairline, moves to the face and upper neck, and proceeds down the body. Diarrhea and ear infections are common complications of measles. More severe complications such as pneumonia and encephalitis, a swelling of the brain, may also occur. The measles virus can live for up to two hours in air where an infected person coughed or sneezed. If other people breathe the contaminated air or touch an infected surface and then touch their eyes, noses, or mouths, they can become infected. People infected with measles can spread it to others from four days before, through four days after, a rash appears. Are measles itchy? Not usually, according to the Cleveland Clinic. How to protect yourself against measles Both ODH and the CDC say the best way to protect yourself against measles is to be vaccinated. USA TODAY reporters James Powel and Mary Walrath-Holdridge contributed to this report. Featured Weekly Ad

美国麻疹病例达到35年最高水平 专家警告疫苗犹豫不决正在助长暴发

US measles cases hit 35-year high as expert warns vaccine hesitancy is fueling the outbreak
新闻简报2026年9月,美国经历了麻疹病例35年来的最高水平,全国范围内报告的病例超过3000例。公共卫生专家泰勒·埃文斯将这一激增归因于日益增长的疫苗犹豫不决,包括来自联邦官员的信号,并警告称政治不应干扰公共卫生决策。宾夕法尼亚州是受影响最严重的州之一,报告超过500例病例,两名儿童被确认死于麻疹,这是美国近年来首次出现此类死亡病例。埃文斯强调了该病毒的高度传染性,指出在100名未接种疫苗的人群中,大约有20人可能感染,远高于流感的传播感染率。他敦促家长了解本地暴发情况,如果附近有感染报告,应让儿童留在家中,并优先考虑疫苗接种。埃文斯还强调了社区领袖需要与科学家和公共卫生专业人士建立联系,以应对疫苗犹豫不决,特别是在缺乏初级医疗服务的地区。

时间

B routine priorityPublic-health news

US measles cases hit 35-year high as expert warns vaccine hesitancy is fueling the outbreak

News Brief
In September 2026, the United States experienced a 35-year high in measles cases, with over 3,000 reported nationwide. Public health expert Tyler Evans attributed the surge to growing vaccine hesitancy, including signals from federal officials, and warned that politics should not interfere with public health decisions. Pennsylvania was among the hardest-hit states, with more than 500 cases and two children confirmed to have died from measles, marking the first such deaths in the U.S. in recent years. Evans highlighted the virus's high infectiousness, noting that in a group of 100 unvaccinated people, about 20 could contract it, far surpassing influenza's transmission rate. He urged parents to stay informed about local outbreaks, keep children home if nearby infections are reported, and prioritize vaccination. Evans also emphasized the need for community leaders to connect with scientists and public health professionals to address vaccine hesitancy, particularly in areas with limited access to primary care.
Original source text (English)
Fri, September 4, 2026 at 5:38 PM UTC Measles infections across the United States have reached a 35-year high, with more than 3,000 cases reported nationwide, and a public health expert says growing vaccine hesitancy — including signals from federal officials — is a driving force behind the surge. Dr. Tyler Evans, CEO of Wellness Equity Alliance and former chief medical officer for New York City, said the scale of the outbreak demands urgent action and warns that politics must be removed from public health decisions. "This is the largest outbreak since 1991," Evans said. "We really have to ensure that politics stays out of public health." Evans said measles is one of the most infectious diseases known. In a room of 100 unvaccinated people, roughly 20 could contract the virus — a transmission rate far exceeding influenza, which spreads to one or two people under similar conditions. That level of infectiousness, he said, makes vaccination the only reliable defense. He said a lack of public confidence in vaccines has been a long-standing challenge, one that accelerated sharply during the COVID-19 pandemic. With hesitancy now being amplified by public figures and federal officials, Evans said the consequences are showing up in case counts. "Microbes don't know the difference" between red states and blue states, Evans said. "Politics really needs to stay out of public health." Evans said community leaders must step in to bridge the gap between scientists and the public, especially in areas where access to primary care is limited and patients may only get 15 minutes with a doctor. "The community leaders need to be connected to the scientists and the public health professionals," Evans said. "It can't always be the doctors." Pennsylvania has become one of the hardest-hit states, surpassing 500 cases. The state has also confirmed two children have died of measles — among the first such deaths reported in the U.S. this year. RELATED STORY | RFK Jr., Pennsylvania officials clash over measles-related deaths Evans dismissed the debate over whether children are dying from measles or merely with it, calling the distinction a dangerous distraction. "When we're dealing with infectious diseases... there's a whole host of factors that contribute to somebody's sickness," Evans said. "But the infectious disease could be the tipping point that really leads them to that hospitalization or that death. And why play around with that? These are babies." He said young children and those with immunocompromising conditions face the greatest risk, as their immune systems are less equipped to fight off the compounding effects of the virus. Evans, who writes about the intersection of public health and politics in his book Pandemics, Poverty and Politics , urged parents to check local case counts, contact school districts about known outbreaks, and keep children home if infections have been reported nearby. RELATED STORY | Measles outbreak reported at ICE facility on Texas military base Above all, he said, get vaccinated. "These vaccines are one of the most effective out there," Evans said. "This is not something that we want to play with."
View five news elements
Time
"This is the largest outbreak since 1991," Evans said.
Location and population
United States, with Pennsylvania as one of the hardest-hit states, and a population at risk including unvaccinated individuals, young children, and those with immunocompromising conditions.
Event
Measles infections have reached a 35-year high in the United States, with over 3,000 cases reported nationwide. A public health expert, Tyler Evans, warned that vaccine hesitancy, including signals from federal officials, is fueling the outbreak. Two children have died from measles, marking the first such deaths in the U.S.
Scale, impact and risk
The outbreak has reached its largest scale since 1991, with over 3,000 cases reported. The measles virus is highly infectious, with a transmission rate of about 20 out of 100 unvaccinated people, far exceeding that of influenza. Young children and immunocompromised individuals are at the greatest risk of severe illness or death.
Response, status and uncertainty
Evans called for urgent action to address vaccine hesitancy and urged parents to check local case counts, contact school districts, and ensure vaccination. He emphasized the need for community leaders to bridge the gap between scientists and the public, especially in areas with limited access to primary care.

麻疹病例突破3000例,达到三十年来最高水平,CDC报告

Measles cases top 3,000 to reach highest level in three decades, CDC reports
新闻简报CDC报告称,美国的麻疹病例已达到3134例,创35年来新高,且麻疹持续了20多个月。在宾夕法尼亚州,两名婴儿死于 麻疹,其中包括一名患有罕见遗传性疾病的 6 周大婴儿和一名母亲被感染的新生儿。暴发 主要集中在兰开斯特县,并已蔓延到威斯康星州和俄亥俄州,那里的病例数大幅上升。CDC 尚未正式承认 2026 年的死亡人数,称需要更多信息。该机构警告说,麻疹 的表现就像是流行病一样,国内 传播 推高了医疗费用和住院率。美国可能会失去 麻疹 消除地位,泛美卫生组织计划在 11 月审查情况。专家强调保持高 疫苗接种 率对于防止进一步传播和保护弱势群体的重要性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
CDC 于 2026 年 9 月 4 日报告了 麻疹 病例,麻疹 持续了至少 20 个月,宾夕法尼亚州的死亡发生在 2026 年 8 月。
地点与对象
暴发 主要位于宾夕法尼亚州,尤其是兰开斯特县,另外 暴发 位于威斯康星州和俄亥俄州。受影响人群包括婴儿、儿童和孕妇,重点是 疫苗接种 率较低的地区。
事件
宾夕法尼亚州验尸官证实,两名婴儿死于 麻疹,美国确诊的 麻疹 病例已达到 35 年来的最高点,达到 3,134 例。CDC正在监测事态发展,美国可能会失去麻疹消除状态。
规模、影响与风险
过去几个月,宾夕法尼亚州至少报告了 577 例病例,上周新增 115 例。暴发 已导致数百人住院并增加医疗费用。CDC 警告说,麻疹 现在的表现就像是一种流行病,有进一步传播的风险,并可能失去消除状态。
应对、状态与不确定性
CDC 正在监测情况,尚未正式承认 2026 年的死亡人数。该机构将在获得更多信息后更新其报告。可能会失去 麻疹 消除状态,泛美卫生组织 (PAHO) 计划于 11 月召开会议来决定。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Measles cases top 3,000 to reach highest level in three decades, CDC reports

News Brief
The CDC reported that measles cases in the U.S. have reached a 35-year high of 3,134, with the outbreak continuing for over 20 months. In Pennsylvania, two infants died from measles, including a 6-week-old baby with a rare genetic disorder and a newborn whose mother was infected. The outbreaks are primarily concentrated in Lancaster County and have spread to Wisconsin and Ohio, where case numbers have risen significantly. The CDC has not officially acknowledged the 2026 deaths, citing the need for more information. The agency warns that measles is acting like it is endemic, with domestic transmission driving up healthcare costs and hospitalizations. The U.S. may lose its measles elimination status, as PAHO plans to review the situation in November. Experts emphasize the importance of maintaining high vaccination rates to prevent further spread and protect vulnerable populations.

Listen to this article with a free profile 00:0000:00 The new measles milestone, the highest number of confirmed cases in the U.S. since 1991, is yet another indication that the highly contagious virus is affecting areas of the country with plummeting vaccination rates.Joe Raedle / Getty Images Share Add to Google Sept. 4, 2026, 6:04 PM EDT By Erika Edwards A 6-week-old baby girl is one of two people with measles who died in Pennsylvania, the Lancaster County coroner said Friday, as the nation’s measles tally hit a new 35-year record of 3,134 cases. Dr. Stephen Diamantoni told NBC affiliate WGAL that the baby died in August and also had a rare genetic disorder called Amish lethal microcephaly. It’s the second measles-related death in an infant in Lancaster County. A baby boy died shortly after birth in mid-August after his infected mother went into labor a month early. At least 115 more people in Pennsylvania, mostly in Lancaster County, have been diagnosed with measles in the last week, the health department said Friday, bringing the state’s outbreak to about 577 cases within the last few months. Based on new data from the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention, measles in the U.S. is still accelerating. Outbreaks in Wisconsin and Ohio are picking up speed. Wisconsin has 144 cases, up from 26 a month ago. And 129 cases are now in Ohio, up from just over 60 in the same time period. The number of people across the country who have gotten sick with measles is undoubtedly much higher because many don’t get tested, experts say. The new milestone, the highest number of confirmed cases since 1991, is yet another indication that the highly contagious virus is taking over, particularly in areas of the country with plummeting vaccination rates. Outbreaks are driving up healthcare costs and have led to hundreds of hospitalizations, including of children and pregnant women. “Measles is acting like it’s endemic now. There’s a lot of domestic transmission,” said Dr. Demetre Daskalakis, who directed the CDC’s National Center for Immunization and Respiratory Diseases before resigning last year. “If I were a betting person, I would expect 5,000-plus cases this year.” The ongoing outbreaks are threatening the country’s status of having eliminated measles in 2000. Nearly everyone who’s had measles caught the virus from a family member, a neighbor or another person within U.S. borders, experts say. Just 16 cases were reported among people visiting from other countries. Global health officials are eyeing the U.S. measles situation carefully. In November, a group within the World Health Organization, the Pan American Health Organization (PAHO), will meet to decide whether the U.S. has lost its measles elimination status. PAHO is looking specifically at whether a single chain of measles transmission has gone on for more than 12 months, as well as whether there are a significant number of cases for which the source of infection is unknown, said Noel Brewer, the chair of an independent committee that assists the CDC in analyzing the country’s measles situation ahead of the PAHO meeting. “The country’s had measles cases every week for the last 20 months, and at least one regional outbreak has already passed the 12-month mark,” said Brewer, also a professor of public health at the University of North Carolina at Chapel Hill. “There’s no end in sight.” Daskalakis likened losing measles elimination status to finding uncontrolled blood pressure during a routine medical checkup: It’s a warning signal of health problems to come. “Measles elimination is more than continuous transmission. It’s also, ‘Do you have a public health system that’s able to respond nimbly? Do you have adequate surveillance? Do you have an environment that’s conducive to high coverage for measles vaccine?’” Daskalakis said. The development would indicate “a loss of faith” in the country’s ability to control outbreaks, said Dr. William Moss, a professor in the departments of epidemiology and international health at the Johns Hopkins Bloomberg School of Public Health. “Getting it back would take a tremendous amount of work,” Moss said. “We would have to stop measles virus transmission in the country, and then undergo a reverification process.” In addition to the Pennsylvania deaths, three others died of complications related to measles in 2025. Those deaths occurred in Texas and New Mexico and were immediately recognized by the CDC. After a public tug-of-war between Pennsylvania Gov. Josh Shapiro, who announced the recent deaths during a briefing last week, and Health Secretary Robert F. Kennedy Jr., who suggested on social media that the deaths had been “fabricated” by one of the governor’s staff members, the CDC hasn’t officially acknowledged the 2026 deaths. On Friday, the CDC referred a request for comment to a post on X from Kennedy saying that the agency “will continue to update its reporting as additional information becomes available.” Share Add to Google Erika Edwards Erika Edwards is a health and medical news writer and reporter for NBC News and "TODAY."
View five news elements
Time
The CDC reported the measles cases on September 4, 2026, with the outbreak continuing for at least 20 months and the Pennsylvania deaths occurring in August 2026.
Location and population
The outbreak is primarily in Pennsylvania, especially Lancaster County, with additional outbreaks in Wisconsin and Ohio. The affected population includes infants, children, and pregnant women, with a focus on areas with low vaccination rates.
Event
Two infants died from measles in Pennsylvania, confirmed by the state’s coroner, and the U.S. has reached a 35-year high of 3,134 confirmed measles cases. The CDC is monitoring the situation, and the U.S. may lose its measles elimination status.
Scale, impact and risk
At least 577 cases have been reported in Pennsylvania within the last few months, with 115 new cases in the last week. The outbreaks have led to hundreds of hospitalizations and increased healthcare costs. The CDC warns that measles is acting like it’s endemic now, with a risk of further spread and potential loss of elimination status.
Response, status and uncertainty
The CDC is monitoring the situation and has not officially acknowledged the 2026 deaths. The agency will update its reporting as additional information becomes available. may lose its measles elimination status, with a meeting planned in November by the Pan American Health Organization (PAHO) to decide.

威斯康星州国土安全部:家庭应与返校学生一起检查 麻疹 疫苗接种

Wisconsin DHS: Families should check measles vaccinations with students back in school

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Wisconsin DHS: Families should check measles vaccinations with students back in school

News Brief
Wisconsin health officials have reported an ongoing measles outbreak as students return to school, urging families to ensure their children are vaccinated. As of September 4, 2026, the state has recorded 144 confirmed and probable measles cases, including two hospitalizations, primarily concentrated in southwestern Wisconsin. Officials emphasize that vaccination rates among kindergarteners remain below the 95% threshold needed to prevent community-wide spread, with only 85.1% up to date on the MMR vaccine during the 2025-26 school year. The Wisconsin Department of Health Services (DHS) is encouraging families to check their children's immunization records through the Wisconsin Immunization Registry or contact their healthcare provider. Those without a regular provider can reach out to their local health department or call 211 for assistance in finding vaccination services. The outbreak highlights the risk of measles transmission in densely populated school settings, where the virus spreads quickly. DHS notes that two doses of the MMR vaccine are about 97% effective at preventing measles.
Original source text (English)

Image courtesy of the US Centers for Disease Control and Prevention. MADISON, Wis. — With students back in classrooms across Wisconsin, state health officials are urging parents to make sure their children are vaccinated against measles as an outbreak continues in parts of the state. As of Friday, Wisconsin Department of Health Services (DHS) says the state has recorded 144 confirmed and probable measles cases so far in 2026, including two hospitalizations. The current outbreak has been concentrated primarily in southwestern Wisconsin, but one case has been confirmed in Waukesha County. Health officials are particularly concerned about vaccination rates as students return to classrooms, where highly contagious illnesses can spread quickly. According to the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention, about 85.1% of Wisconsin kindergarteners were up to date on the measles, mumps and rubella — or MMR — vaccine during the 2025-26 school year. That’s a slight increase from the previous year, but remains well below the roughly 95% vaccination level generally needed to prevent measles from spreading within a community. “Children spend a lot of time together in the classroom which means diseases can spread quickly in schools,” said Wisconsin Immunization Program Director Dr. Stephanie Schauer. “Higher vaccination coverage can help prevent a single case in a school from turning into a community-wide outbreak.” DHS says two doses of the MMR vaccine are about 97% effective at preventing measles. State health officials are encouraging families to check their children’s records through the Wisconsin Immunization Registry or contact their healthcare provider. Families without a regular provider can also contact their local health department or call 211 for help finding vaccination services. 兰开斯特县验尸官称 麻疹 导致第二例婴儿死亡 美国病例数已突破 3,100

Lancaster County coroner says measles caused second infant death as US cases soar past 3,100
新闻简报在宾夕法尼亚州兰开斯特县,验尸官史蒂芬·迪亚蒙托尼博士于 2026 年 8 月 18 日确认了一例与 麻疹 相关的婴儿死亡。这标志着该县第二例此类死亡,第一例被归因于脾破裂而非 麻疹。美国疾病控制与预防中心(CDC)未将这些死亡纳入其 2026 年 麻疹 死亡统计。截至 2026 年 8 月 26 日,美国 麻疹 病例总数达到 3,134 例,宾夕法尼亚州新增 115 例。8% 的患者被收治入院,94% 的病例涉及未接种疫苗或未知 疫苗接种 状态的个体。宾夕法尼亚州卫生部警告称,宾夕法尼亚州收费公路的新斯坦顿服务区可能存在潜在暴露风险。该病例最初由特殊儿童诊所管理,随后转交验尸官办公室。婴儿感染时间线的不确定性仍未解决,更广泛的公共卫生应对措施包括呼吁旅行者在劳动节周末前保持警惕。

时间
宾夕法尼亚州卫生部上周确认了两例与 麻疹 相关的死亡,美国疾病控制与预防中心(CDC)于 2026 年 8 月 26 日更新了病例统计。第二例婴儿死亡发生在 2026 年 8 月 18 日。

兰开斯特县验尸官史蒂芬·迪亚蒙托尼博士确认,麻疹 导致该县第二例婴儿死亡,该病例最初被报告为 '与 麻疹 相关'。美国疾病控制与预防中心(CDC)未将这些死亡纳入其 2026 年 麻疹 死亡统计。
